# Core language test gaps and PostgreSQL 17 comparison

The review covered all 25 chapters of the [Musoq Core SQL Language Specification](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md), 329 numbered features, 217 diagnostic identifiers, 20 explicit library contracts and all 165 named grammar productions. The audit baseline was `5a916e949b4fe956483523b13617702823694e0d`. The tests and fixes below are present on the local implementation branch. PostgreSQL comparisons use reasoned expectations from the version 17 manual. All executed SQL in this report ran on Musoq.

Five engine defects were reproduced, fixed and regression-tested. Start with [remaining decisions](#remaining-decisions) for behavior that still differs, [the feature register](#wave-register) for exact outcomes, or [compatibility qualification](#compatibility-qualification) for the independent datasource checks.

## Engine defects reproduced on the audit baseline

1. **Infix `NOT BETWEEN` (116), fixed.** Core grammar accepts `[NOT] BETWEEN`. On the audit baseline, both `11 NOT BETWEEN 1 AND 10` and `11 NOT BETWEEN NULL AND 10` fail in an inline SELECT with `MQ2004`, even though the query has a FROM clause. The closely related `NOT (11 BETWEEN NULL AND 10)` returns true. PostgreSQL predicts true for both infix cases. The corrected parser uses the existing negated range expression. The result and diagnostic assertions are recorded in wave 116. [Core grammar](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md#L7639) · [Parser implementation](src/dotnet/Musoq.Parser/Parser.Expressions.Predicates.cs#L66) · [PostgreSQL comparisons](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/functions-comparison.html)

2. **Bare NULL operands with all four pattern operators (117–120), fixed.** The helper-level NULL tests supply a typed string NULL. On the audit baseline the SQL probe `SELECT NULL LIKE 'x' FROM values {(Seed: 0)} s` failed during compilation with `MQ9001`. Each of `NOT LIKE`, `RLIKE`, and `NOT RLIKE` failed in its own equivalent query. A string column containing NULL succeeds and returns `false, true, false, true`. The SQL-level regressions compare bare NULL with the typed control and check all four outputs. PostgreSQL predicts NULL for each analogous operator, including regex search expressed with `~` and `!~`. The corrected C# target gives false, true, false and true for the four literal expressions, in order. Existing datasource queries keep their two-valued semantics. [Core NULL results](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md#L6702) · [Helper-level NULL tests](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/OperatorsNullHandlingTests.cs#L114) · [PostgreSQL pattern matching](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/functions-matching.html)

3. **Unsigned 64-bit shifts with an integer count (026–027), fixed.** The SQL expression `n >> 64` for an unsigned 64-bit column failed to bind with `MQ3007`. Both shifts now determine the result type from the promoted left operand. A wide count uses its low bits. PostgreSQL 17 has signed integer carriers. Its integer types do not include a direct uint64 counterpart. [Exact values and column types](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NumericBoundaryResultTests.cs#L28) · [Wide and nullable counts](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NumericBoundaryResultTests.cs#L49) · [PostgreSQL numeric types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/datatype-numeric.html)

4. **Nullable comparison keys under DISTINCT and GROUP BY (099), fixed.** `SELECT DISTINCT x = 1 FROM values {(x:1),(x:2),(x:null)} v` failed in the native compiler with `MQ9001`. The ordinary projection returned true, false and NULL. The aggregate now takes its key type from the bound comparison expression, preserving the nullable result for both single and composite keys. PostgreSQL 17 predicts the same three distinct values. [Distinct scalar keys](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/ComparisonGroupingKeyTests.cs#L13) · [Group counts](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/ComparisonGroupingKeyTests.cs#L37) · [PostgreSQL select lists](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/queries-select-lists.html#QUERIES-DISTINCT)

5. **CTE names adjacent to their output list (265–266), fixed.** `WITH c(Y) AS (...)` previously failed with MQ2001 while `WITH c (Y) AS (...)` succeeded. Both forms now use the same CTE output list, also for recursive and chained declarations. PostgreSQL 17 accepts both spellings. [Chained output columns](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/CteColumnListResultTests.cs#L14) · [Recursive output](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/CteColumnListResultTests.cs#L26) · [PostgreSQL WITH](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/queries-with.html)

## Original assertion gaps now covered

| Boundary | Result the regression must preserve |
|---|---|
| Composite full outer join | Both sides contain key 1. A second component selects the single (x, x) pair. The left and right payloads each appear once on their unmatched side with the opposite side NULL. Both hash and forced nested-loop plans must return exactly these three rows and the nullable column types. A companion fixture checks all four combinations when each side repeats the same join key twice. [Composite-key results](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/Join.FullOuterJoinTests.cs#L175) · [Duplicate pairings](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/Join.FullOuterJoinTests.cs#L74) |
| Inclusive and negated ranges | A known upper bound can decide false even when the lower bound is NULL. The positive boundary fixture requires false, NULL and true in one row. [BETWEEN results](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/RangePredicateResultTests.cs#L46) |
| Empty and all-NULL numeric aggregates | Count(*), Count(x), Sum, Avg, Min and Max return one complete row in both cases. On empty input it is (0, 0, NULL, NULL, NULL, NULL). With one NULL row only the first count changes to 1. Six column-type assertions protect the schema. [Aggregate results and types](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/GlobalAggregateBoundaryTests.cs#L13) |
| Shifts and masked counts | Left and right shifts check both the value and its CLR carrier, count masking at the 32- and 64-bit boundaries, and NULL counts. [Shift results](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NumericBoundaryResultTests.cs#L12) |

## Remaining decisions

1. **Align or explicitly retain operation-dependent NULL and NaN behavior.** A script can change its result by moving a nullable comparison from LET to SELECT, or by changing NaN membership from a literal list to a materialized subquery. These are the highest-priority consistency decisions. Examples, current outputs and migration implications appear below.
2. **Decide which PostgreSQL differences merit a versioned migration.** Plain set-operation multiplicity and precedence, default NULL sorting, and NULL pattern matching can change rows, pages, ranks or filters. Each currently has a regression guard. The table below records the narrower changes for ordinality and ORDER BY positions as well.
3. **Improve diagnostic precision without silently reassigning public codes.** MQ2013 highlights the closing parenthesis after an invalid CTE column. MQ3022 can identify an unresolved method owner for `Length()` across two sources, while `a.Length()` identifies the arity as MQ3087. Consider locating the bad CTE entry and distinguishing a known arity error in multi-source calls. Six registered identifiers have catalog coverage without an identified query emitter. They are MQ2023, MQ2026, MQ2028, MQ2029, MQ4009 and MQ4010. Choose whether to publish triggers or document them as inactive before promising SQL-level behavior. [MQ2013 and owner examples](#published-diagnostics)

### Specification and compatibility decisions

| Behavior | Reasoned result and proposed disposition |
|---|---|
| Plain `EXCEPT` and `INTERSECT` | For left `[1,1,2,2]` and right `[2]`, Musoq specifies `[1,1]` for EXCEPT and `[2,2]` for INTERSECT. PostgreSQL plain forms imply `[1]` and `[2]`. PostgreSQL INTERSECT ALL also returns one `2` with that one-row right input, so it does not replace Musoq membership. Current tests directly protect duplicate retention. Preserve it while deciding whether the plain forms should align in a future version. A migration would need a documented membership rewrite for users relying on duplicates. [Musoq specification](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md#L5359) · [Bare INTERSECT example](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/SetsOperatorsTests.IntersectMultiplicity.cs#L13) · [PostgreSQL set operations](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/queries-union.html) |
| Set precedence | Musoq uses left association in mixed chains. For `1 UNION 2 INTERSECT 2`, left association yields `[2]`, while PostgreSQL’s INTERSECT precedence gives `[1,2]`. Existing tests protect Musoq’s left association. Consider PostgreSQL precedence as a future compatibility change with migration examples using explicit operand grouping. [Musoq specification](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md#L5405) · [Existing test](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/Core027SetOperationTests.cs#L92) · [PostgreSQL set precedence](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/queries-union.html) |
| Default NULL sort placement | Ordering `[1,NULL,2]` ascending yields `[NULL,1,2]` under Musoq rules and `[1,2,NULL]` under PostgreSQL defaults. The difference also affects windows with nullable order keys. Both use equivalent explicit `NULLS FIRST/LAST` syntax. Consider aligning defaults only with a versioned policy because it can change pages, ranks, and ASOF candidate choices. [Musoq specification](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md#L4942) · [Existing test](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/Core028OrderByTests.cs#L14) · [PostgreSQL sorting](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/queries-order.html) |
| NULL pattern predicates | Musoq `NULL LIKE 'x'` is false and negation is true. PostgreSQL gives unknown in both cases. Consequently `WHERE x NOT LIKE 'x'` retains a NULL row in Musoq and drops it in PostgreSQL. Record the difference with the existing two-valued Musoq pattern contract. Consider adopting three-valued semantics only through an explicit compatibility decision and include RLIKE and multi-field quantifiers in the migration analysis. [Musoq specification](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md#L6702) · [Nearest tests](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/OperatorsNullHandlingTests.cs#L114) · [PostgreSQL pattern matching](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/functions-matching.html) |
| Float NaN policy | Two NaNs and one finite value produce one NaN group of size two. DISTINCT returns one NaN. Self-UNION returns four NaNs, EXCEPT keeps the two left NaNs and INTERSECT keeps only the common finite value. Scalar equality and list membership reject NaN; materialized subquery membership matches it. Musoq sorts NaN before finite values. PostgreSQL 17 treats NaNs as equal and sorts them after finite values. Keep the existing operation-specific behavior for compatibility. Aligning equality across membership forms would change results and requires a versioned decision. [Set-operation results](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/FloatingPointNaNResultTests.cs#L49) · [Membership and comparison](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/FloatingPointNaNResultTests.cs#L13) · [PostgreSQL numeric types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/datatype-numeric.html#DATATYPE-FLOAT) |
| NULL in scalar `LET` initializers | `NULL = 1` initializes to false, `NULL = NULL` to true, and `5 BETWEEN NULL AND 10` to true. The same three projections return NULL. A nullable Boolean initializer using `FALSE AND NULL` reports MQ3065. Its projection returns false. LET uses a separate constant evaluator that orders NULL below a number. Moving comparisons to SELECT gives SQL truth values today. Aligning LET would change successful scripts and may make non-nullable declarations invalid. A versioned policy needs tests for both declaration nullabilities and for provider arguments computed from old scripts. [Side-by-side values](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/ScriptInitializerComparisonTests.cs#L20) · [Bind and projection controls](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/ScriptInitializerComparisonTests.cs#L37) · [PostgreSQL comparisons](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/functions-comparison.html) · [PostgreSQL logic](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/functions-logical.html) |
| Ordinality | The first value emitted by Musoq APPLY has ordinal `0` of type int. PostgreSQL table functions start at `1` of type bigint. This is a documented Musoq convention. Keep it and document the explicit subtraction needed when porting queries. [Musoq specification](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md#L3568) · [Existing test](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/Core021ApplyAdvancedTests.cs#L16) · [PostgreSQL table functions](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/queries-table-expressions.html#QUERIES-TABLEFUNCTIONS) |
| `ORDER BY 1` | PostgreSQL sorts by the first select-list expression. Musoq documents and tests a dedicated rejection. This is a candidate for an additive compatibility change after checking how it interacts with expression syntax. [Musoq specification](specs/musoq-core-language-spec.md#L5610) · [Existing test](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/Core028OrderByTests.cs#L156) · [PostgreSQL sorting](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/queries-order.html) |

| 027 Right shift | Unsigned 32- and 64-bit right shifts by their full width reproduce the input. A count of one gives 2147483647u and 9223372036854775807ul. Signed -8 shifts to -4. A 4294967297ul count preserves the low bit, promotes an eight-bit value to int and propagates a NULL count in both directions. | 034 Floating-point domains | Two NaNs and one finite value produce one NaN group of size two. DISTINCT returns one NaN. Self-UNION returns four NaNs, EXCEPT keeps the two left NaNs and INTERSECT keeps only the common finite value. Scalar equality and list membership reject NaN; materialized subquery membership matches it. [Set-operation results](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/FloatingPointNaNResultTests.cs#L49) · [Membership and comparison](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/FloatingPointNaNResultTests.cs#L13) · [Grouping and sorting](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Relational.Tests/FloatingPointNaNResultTests.cs#L33). | PostgreSQL treats NaN as equal to NaN and sorts it above ordinary numbers. Musoq uses context-specific NaN equality and places it below in numeric ordering. Make the divergence explicit and decide the desired policy per operation before any changes. [Floating point](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/datatype-numeric.html#DATATYPE-FLOAT) |
| 035 Decimal domains | Decimal inputs -3, -1, 1 and 3 units at scale 28 give -2, 0, 0 and 2 units when halved. A separate column value one above the decimal maximum reports MQ7018 during execution. [DecimalDivision_ShouldRoundScaleReductionTiesToEven](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NumericBoundaryResultTests.cs#L72). | Finite in-domain values are comparable. PostgreSQL numeric has a broader domain and rounds midpoint ties away from zero. Musoq decimal uses its narrower domain and ties-to-even rules. [Numeric types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/datatype-numeric.html) |

### Published diagnostics

This register covers 217 identifiers. For 147 query-analysis pairs the tests require one specific diagnostic on the trigger and no diagnostics on its repair. Four strict-compilation repairs return the complete expected row, respectively 1, 5, 2147483647 and 2 for MQ2036, MQ3008, MQ3032 and MQ3097. Another 49 codes have dedicated provider, runtime, schema or compiler-boundary tests and controls. Eight unassigned warning numbers, three retired or intentionally quiet codes and six catalog entries without an identified query emitter are recorded individually. PostgreSQL comparison uses SQLSTATE only where inputs are equivalent. [PostgreSQL error codes](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/errcodes-appendix.html)

### Explicit library contracts

The SQL suite executes 60 cases across these 20 entries, including text or character operands, nullable numeric carriers, byte arrays and delimiters where documented. These results depend on a provider exposing the signature. For PostgreSQL comparisons, use explicit case folding or case-insensitive matching where appropriate. PostgreSQL substring positions use characters. The radix, byte and Base64 calls require comparison of the encoded result with explicit input type, byte order and encoding. PostgreSQL string_agg skips NULL inputs. To express Musoq's one-argument form, substitute empty text for each NULL input before aggregation. [PostgreSQL string functions](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/functions-string.html) · [PostgreSQL aggregates](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/functions-aggregate.html)

| Library contract | Specified behavior | Assertions and execution evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Contains(string, string) | Returns case-insensitive containment or NULL when either input is NULL. Both arguments are evaluated in order. | SQL fixture checks all three case variants by returned row. [Where_ContainsCaseInsensitive_MatchesDifferentCases](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/StringComparisonConsistencyTests.OrderingAndFunctions.cs#L126) (1 passed). Contains finds a mixed-case internal match, rejects an absent needle and returns NULL for either missing argument. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L19). |
| StartsWith(string, string) | Returns case-insensitive prefix matching or NULL when either input is NULL. Both arguments are evaluated in order. | Checks exact rows matching a case-varied prefix. [Where_StartsWithCaseInsensitive_MatchesDifferentCases](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/StringComparisonConsistencyTests.OrderingAndFunctions.cs#L151) (1 passed). StartsWith accepts the mixed-case prefix, rejects the same characters in the middle and propagates either NULL argument. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L31). |
| EndsWith(string, string) | Uses case-insensitive suffix matching. | Checks exact rows matching a case-varied suffix. [Where_EndsWithCaseInsensitive_MatchesDifferentCases](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/StringComparisonConsistencyTests.OrderingAndFunctions.cs#L176) (1 passed). EndsWith accepts the mixed-case suffix, rejects an internal substring and propagates either NULL argument. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L43). |
| Replace(string, string, string) | Uses a case-insensitive search for the replacement target. | Library fixture checks complete replacement text across all three case variants. [Replace_CaseInsensitive_ReplacesAllOccurrences](src/dotnet/Musoq.Plugins.Functions.Tests/StringCultureConsistencyTests.ReplacementAndCasing.cs#L13) (1 passed). Replacing a in aBcA gives xBcx. Missing search or replacement leaves the input intact, and missing input yields NULL. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L55). |
| IndexOf(string, string) | Returns the ordinal-ignore-case match index. | Three fixtures independently require position 6. [Select_IndexOfCaseInsensitive](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/StringComparisonConsistencyTests.OrderingAndFunctions.cs#L244) (1 passed). IndexOf finds B at UTF-16 position 3 in A😀Bc, returns -1 for a missing substring and NULL for either missing argument. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L67). |
| ToLower(string) | Normalizes case for grouping and deduplication. | Checks both transformed outputs including Polish text. [Select_ToLowerUsesInvariantCulture](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/StringComparisonConsistencyTests.OrderingAndFunctions.cs#L208) (1 passed). Invariant lowercasing returns żółć for ŻÓŁĆ, an empty string for empty input and NULL for missing input. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L79). |
| ToUpper(string) | Normalizes case for grouping and deduplication. | Checks both transformed outputs including Polish text. [Select_ToUpperUsesInvariantCulture](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/StringComparisonConsistencyTests.OrderingAndFunctions.cs#L191) (1 passed). Invariant uppercasing returns ŻÓŁĆ for żółć, an empty string for empty input and NULL for missing input. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L85). |
| Substring(string, …) | Uses UTF-16 units for text positions. | Checks ordinary substring boundaries and NULL input. [SubstrTest](src/dotnet/Musoq.Plugins.Functions.Tests/Strings.BasicTests.cs#L11) (1 passed). The two-argument form takes a prefix length. The three-argument form takes a zero-based UTF-16 index and length; a one-unit cut may split a surrogate pair. Each missing argument has a separate assertion. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L91). |
| ToHex(nullable numeric) | A value converts like its underlying type and NULL yields NULL. | Individual nullable UInt16 entries expect FFFF for MaxValue and NULL for NULL. [String_Cases_ReturnExpected](src/dotnet/Musoq.Plugins.Conversions.Tests/ConvertingTests.cs#L71) (90 passed). ToHex is checked for a present 42 and a typed NULL in every signed and unsigned integral width, decimal, single and double. Hex expectations explicitly cover the underlying byte widths. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L131). |
| ToBin(nullable numeric) | A value converts like its underlying type and NULL yields NULL. | Data cases independently expect 101010 for 42 and NULL for typed NULL. [RadixConversion_ShouldPreservePresentAndMissingValues](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableConversionBindingTests.cs#L16) (10 passed). ToBin is checked for a present 42 and a typed NULL in every signed and unsigned integral width, decimal, single and double. The independent values distinguish integral radix output, unsupported domains and the double bit representation. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L152). |
| ToOcta(nullable numeric) | A value converts like its underlying type and NULL yields NULL. | Data cases independently expect 52 for 42 and NULL for typed NULL. [RadixConversion_ShouldPreservePresentAndMissingValues](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableConversionBindingTests.cs#L16) (10 passed). ToOcta is checked for a present 42 and a typed NULL in every signed and unsigned integral width, decimal, single and double. The independent values distinguish integral radix output, unsupported domains and the double bit representation. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L173). |
| ToDec(nullable numeric) | A value converts like its underlying type and NULL yields NULL. | Data cases independently expect 42 text and NULL for typed NULL. [RadixConversion_ShouldPreservePresentAndMissingValues](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableConversionBindingTests.cs#L16) (10 passed). ToDec is checked for a present 42 and a typed NULL in every signed and unsigned integral width, decimal, single and double. The independent values distinguish integral radix output, unsupported domains and the double bit representation. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L194). |
| ToBytes(nullable Boolean) | Use the Boolean overload’s byte representation and preserve NULL. | Encoded result is 01 for true and NULL for typed NULL. [ToBytes_ShouldUseTheRequestedWidth](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableConversionBindingTests.cs#L32) (15 passed). Nullable Boolean conversion yields bytes 01 for true and 00 for false, and preserves a typed NULL. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L204). |
| ToBytes(nullable 16-bit integer) | Signed and unsigned inputs use the underlying overload’s width and preserve NULL. | Encoded result is FFFF for extreme signed and unsigned cases with typed-NULL controls. [ToBytes_ShouldUseTheRequestedWidth](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableConversionBindingTests.cs#L32) (15 passed). Nullable Int16 and UInt16 retain two bytes, checked by 0201 and 0180. Both typed NULLs propagate. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L215). |
| ToBytes(nullable 32-bit integer) | Signed and unsigned inputs use the underlying overload’s width and preserve NULL. | Encoded result is FFFFFFFF for extreme signed and unsigned cases with typed-NULL controls. [ToBytes_ShouldUseTheRequestedWidth](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableConversionBindingTests.cs#L32) (15 passed). Nullable Int32 and UInt32 retain four bytes, checked by 04030201 and 01000080. Both typed NULLs propagate. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L227). |
| ToBytes(nullable 64-bit integer) | Signed and unsigned inputs use the underlying overload’s width and preserve NULL. | Encoded result is FFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF for extreme signed and unsigned cases with typed-NULL controls. [ToBytes_ShouldUseTheRequestedWidth](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableConversionBindingTests.cs#L32) (15 passed). Nullable Int64 and UInt64 retain eight bytes in little-endian order. The sign-bit and typed-NULL fixtures distinguish both overloads. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L239). |
| GetBytes(string, length, offset) | Use UTF-8 encoding after zero-based substring extraction and propagate NULL. | Exact C3A9, zero-length output and NULL in each argument position. [GetBytes_ShouldEncodeTheRequestedSlice](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableConversionBindingTests.cs#L53) (6 passed). The integer and nullable bounds overloads slice UTF-16 before encoding, yielding C3A9 for é and F09F9880 for 😀. Zero length and NULL in each argument position are checked. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L251). |
| ToBase64(value, offset, length) | Propagate NULL from any nullable argument and preserve the underlying integer-bound behavior. | Checks AQI= and NULL for a nullable length expression using real SQL. [NullableSliceArithmetic_ShouldBindTheNullableOverloadAndPreserveNulls](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/NullableBase64BindingTests.cs#L38) (2 passed). The integer and nullable bounds overloads encode B as Qg== and BC as QkM=. Zero-byte slices and NULL byte array, offset and length are checked separately. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L272). |
| AggregateValues(column) | NULL becomes an empty slot in comma-separated output. | Exact hello,,world proves preservation of the empty entry. [AggregateValuesStringKernel_PreservesNullsAsEmptyEntries](src/dotnet/Musoq.Plugins.Functions.Tests/AggregateValuesTests.cs#L17) (1 passed). Text and nullable-character overloads both give A,,B on A, NULL, B. The result check covers the full concatenated string and its declared type. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L293). |
| AggregateValues(column, delimiter) | NULL values are ignored when using an explicit delimiter. | Exact hello \| world proves the NULL slot is skipped and the delimiter is used. [AggregateValuesStringDelimitedKernel_SkipsNullsAndUsesDelimiter](src/dotnet/Musoq.Plugins.Functions.Tests/AggregateValuesTests.cs#L51) (1 passed). Text and nullable-character overloads both give A/B on A, NULL, B. The result check covers the full concatenated string and its declared type. [SQL results for the overloads](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/CoreLibraryResultTests.cs#L310). |

## Specification reconciliation

The §24 reconciliation corrected the published productions for string concatenation at additive precedence, direct paging references, script defaults, constant distinctness and negated ranges, and static expressions in VALUES. The source notation also identifies the datasource prefix. A VALUES field can calculate `1 < 2`, `5 ^ 3`, or a nullable fallback. CASE and BETWEEN in that position produce MQ3055. A scalar parameter accepts `-1` as a default and rejects `1+2` at parse time. [Typed VALUES outputs](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/InlineValuesGrammarTests.cs#L13) · [Field diagnostic](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/InlineValuesGrammarTests.cs#L35) · [Parameter defaults](src/dotnet/Musoq.Evaluator.Query.Tests/InlineValuesGrammarTests.cs#L47).

The operator, source and expression branches identify their responsible feature even where several alternatives share one test method.

## Formal grammar and owning feature checks

The register covers all 165 named productions in §24. Each top-level alternative has a feature reference. Repeated and optional terms stay in their published position. Each numbered feature above links to the assertions that exercise it. A satellite declaration delegates its syntax to the enabled profile.

## Remediation baseline

The implementation starts at Core `5a916e949b4fe956483523b13617702823694e0d`. The independent DataSources checkout is `d2faf1d44a6518a2af975dd76cde3dd483517110` with its released Core 17.0.22 package pins. Its Release solution run recorded 2271 passing tests, 34 skipped tests and 0 failures across 22 projects. The local identity and outcome register is `.artifacts/core-sql-remediation/ds-baseline-trx.cases.json`.
